 def visualize_attention(model, dataset, device, step, out_dir):                
     model.eval()                                                               
     attention_patterns = []                                                    
                                                                                
     for _ in range(5):  # Get 5 random examples                                
         x, y, _ = dataset.fetch_val_example()                                  
         x = torch.tensor(x).unsqueeze(0).to(device)                            
                                                                                
         with torch.no_grad():                                                  
             output = model(x)                                                  
             # Assuming the last layer's attention is what we want to visualize 
             attention =                                                        
 model.model[0].self_attn.attention_weights.squeeze(0).cpu().numpy()            
                                                                                
         attention_patterns.append(attention)                                   
                                                                                
     # Plot attention patterns                                                  
     fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 5, figsize=(20, 4))                            
     for i, attn in enumerate(attention_patterns):                              
         sns.heatmap(attn, ax=axes[i], cmap='viridis')                          
         axes[i].set_title(f'Example {i+1}')                                    
                                                                                
     plt.tight_layout()                                                         
     plt.savefig(os.path.join(out_dir, f'attention_pattern_step_{step}.png'))   
     plt.close()                                                                
                                                                                
 def run(out_dir, dataset, seed_offset):                                        
     os.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)                                        
     device = torch.device("cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")      
     torch.manual_seed(1337 + seed_offset)                                      
     train_loader, val_loader, n_vocab, n_output = get_data(                    
         operation=dataset,                                                     
         prime=97,                                                              
         training_fraction=0.5,                                                 
         batch_size=512,                                                        
     )                                                                          
                                                                                
     # Create the dataset object                                                
     dataset_obj = operation_mod_p_data(dataset, 97, 0.5)                       
                                                                                
     model = Transformer(                                                       
         num_layers=2,                                                          
         dim_model=128,                                                         
         num_heads=4,                                                           
         vocab_size=n_vocab,                                                    
         output_size=n_output,                                                  
         seq_len=5,                                                             
     ).to(device)                                                               
                                                                                
     optimizer = torch.optim.AdamW(                                             
         model.parameters(),                                                    
         lr=1e-3,                                                               
         betas=(0.9, 0.98),                                                     
         weight_decay=0.5,                                                      
     )                                                                          
     num_train_batches = 10                                                     
     num_eval_batches = 8                                                       
     num_total_updates = 7500                                                   
     warmup_steps = 50                                                          
     scheduler = torch.optim.lr_scheduler.LambdaLR(                             
         optimizer, lr_lambda=lambda s: min(s / warmup_steps, 1)                
     )                                                                          
                                                                                
     final_info, train_log_info, val_log_info = [], [], []                      
     step_val_acc_99 = num_total_updates                                        
     invariance_scores = []                                                     
     max_invariance_score = 0                                                   
     invariance_point = num_total_updates                                       
     grokking_point = num_total_updates                                         
                                                                                
     # Visualize attention at the start (pre-grokking)                          
     visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device, 0, out_dir)                
                                                                                
     for ep in range(num_total_updates // num_train_batches):                   
         train_metrics = train(                                                 
             model,                                                             
             train_loader,                                                      
             optimizer,                                                         
             scheduler,                                                         
             device,                                                            
             num_train_batches,                                                 
         )                                                                      
         val_metrics = evaluate(                                                
             model,                                                             
             val_loader,                                                        
             device,                                                            
             num_eval_batches,                                                  
         )                                                                      
         train_metrics["step"] = (ep + 1) * num_train_batches                   
         val_metrics["step"] = (ep + 1) * num_train_batches                     
                                                                                
         if (ep + 1) * num_train_batches % 100 == 0:                            
             inv_score = invariance_score(model, dataset_obj, device)           
             invariance_scores.append({"step": (ep + 1) * num_train_batches,    
 "score": inv_score})                                                           
             if inv_score > max_invariance_score:                               
                 max_invariance_score = inv_score                               
             if invariance_point == num_total_updates and inv_score > 0.9 *     
 max_invariance_score:                                                          
                 invariance_point = (ep + 1) * num_train_batches                
                 # Visualize attention at invariance point                      
                 visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device,                
 invariance_point, out_dir)                                                     
                                                                                
         if step_val_acc_99 == num_total_updates and val_metrics["val_accuracy" 
 > 0.99:                                                                        
             step_val_acc_99 = val_metrics["step"]                              
             grokking_point = step_val_acc_99                                   
             # Visualize attention at grokking point                            
             visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device, grokking_point,    
 out_dir)                                                                       
                                                                                
         train_log_info.append(train_metrics)                                   
         val_log_info.append(val_metrics)                                       
                                                                                
     # Visualize attention at the end (post-grokking)                           
     visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device, num_total_updates, out_dir 
                                                                                
     final_info = {                                                             
         "final_train_loss": train_metrics["train_loss"],                       
         "final_val_loss": val_metrics["val_loss"],                             
         "final_train_acc": train_metrics["train_accuracy"],                    
         "final_val_acc": val_metrics["val_accuracy"],                          
         "step_val_acc_99": step_val_acc_99,                                    
         "invariance_point": invariance_point,                                  
         "grokking_point": grokking_point,                                      
     }                                                                          
     print(final_info)                                                          
     with open(                                                                 
         os.path.join(out_dir, f"final_info_{dataset}_{seed_offset}.json"), "w" 
     ) as f:                                                                    
         json.dump(final_info, f)

 f"invariance_scores_{dataset}_{seed_offset}.json"), "w"                        
     ) as f:                                                                    
         json.dump(invariance_scores, f)                                        
                                                                                
     return final_info, train_log_info, val_log_info, invariance_scores

Now, we need to modify the Transformer class to store attention weights:        

                                                                                
 <<<<<<< SEARCH                                                                 
 class DecoderBlock(torch.nn.Module):                                           
     def __init__(self, dim_model: int, n_heads: int):                          
         super().__init__()                                                     
                                                                                
         self.self_attn = nn.MultiheadAttention(dim_model, n_heads)             
         self.self_attn_norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim_model)                          
         self.ffn = nn.Sequential(                                              
             nn.Linear(dim_model, dim_model * 4),                               
             nn.GELU(),                                                         
             nn.Linear(dim_model * 4, dim_model),                               
         )                                                                      
         self.ffn_norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim_model)                                
                                                                                
     def forward(self, x: Tensor):                                              
         attn_mask = torch.full(                                                
             (len(x), len(x)), -float("Inf"), device=x.device, dtype=x.dtype    
         )                                                                      
         attn_mask = torch.triu(attn_mask, diagonal=1)                          
                                                                                
         a1, _ = self.self_attn(x, x, x, attn_mask=attn_mask)                   
         a1 = self.self_attn_norm(x + a1)                                       
         a2 = self.ffn(a1)                                                      
         a2 = self.ffn_norm(a1 + a2)

 class DecoderBlock(torch.nn.Module):                                           
     def __init__(self, dim_model: int, n_heads: int):                          
         super().__init__()                                                     
                                                                                
         self.self_attn = nn.MultiheadAttention(dim_model, n_heads)             
         self.self_attn_norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim_model)                          
         self.ffn = nn.Sequential(                                              
             nn.Linear(dim_model, dim_model * 4),                               
             nn.GELU(),                                                         
             nn.Linear(dim_model * 4, dim_model),                               
         )                                                                      
         self.ffn_norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim_model)                                
         self.attention_weights = None                                          
                                                                                
     def forward(self, x: Tensor):                                              
         attn_mask = torch.full(                                                
             (len(x), len(x)), -float("Inf"), device=x.device, dtype=x.dtype    
         )                                                                      
         attn_mask = torch.triu(attn_mask, diagonal=1)                          
                                                                                
         a1, attention_weights = self.self_attn(x, x, x, attn_mask=attn_mask)   
         self.attention_weights = attention_weights                             
         a1 = self.self_attn_norm(x + a1)                                       
         a2 = self.ffn(a1)                                                      
         a2 = self.ffn_norm(a1 + a2)                                            
                                                                                
         return a2

```python
Observations:
1. Invariance Point: For all datasets, the invariance point is consistently reached very early in training (step 100). This suggests that the model quickly learns the task-specific invariances, regardless of the operation type.

2. Grokking Point:
   - x_div_y: Reaches 99% validation accuracy at step 4276 (mean), showing a significant delay between invariance learning and generalization.
   - x_minus_y: Similar to x_div_y, with 99% validation accuracy at step 4533 (mean).
   - x_plus_y: Fastest to reach high validation accuracy, with 99% at step 2480 (mean).
   - permutation: Fails to reach high validation accuracy within the 7500 steps, indicating difficulty in generalizing for this task.

3. Final Performance:
   - x_div_y, x_minus_y, and x_plus_y all achieve perfect or near-perfect validation accuracy by the end of training.
   - The permutation task shows poor generalization, with final validation accuracy at only 11.98%.

4. Training Dynamics:
   - The arithmetic operations (division, subtraction, addition) show similar patterns of rapid invariance learning followed by a period of low validation accuracy, then sudden improvement (grokking).
   - The permutation task exhibits a different pattern, with rapid invariance learning but failure to achieve high validation accuracy.

5. Correlation between Invariance Score and Validation Accuracy:
   - For the arithmetic operations, there is a significant time gap between achieving high invariance scores and high validation accuracy.
   - This suggests that while invariance learning is necessary for generalization, it is not sufficient on its own to guarantee high performance on the validation set.

Next steps: 
1. Analyze the attention patterns at key points in training (pre-grokking, invariance point, grokking point, post-grokking) to understand how learned invariances manifest in the model's attention mechanisms.
2. Investigate why the permutation task is particularly challenging for the model to generalize, despite learning invariances quickly.
3. Experiment with different model architectures or training regimes to see if we can reduce the gap between invariance learning and generalization.
```

```python
def run(out_dir, dataset, seed_offset):
    os.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)
    device = torch.device("cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")
    torch.manual_seed(1337 + seed_offset)
    train_loader, val_loader, n_vocab, n_output = get_data(
        operation=dataset,
        prime=97,
        training_fraction=0.5,
        batch_size=512,
    )

    # Create the dataset object
    dataset_obj = operation_mod_p_data(dataset, 97, 0.5)

    model = Transformer(
        num_layers=2,
        dim_model=128,
        num_heads=4,
        vocab_size=n_vocab,
        output_size=n_output,
        seq_len=5,
    ).to(device)

    optimizer = torch.optim.AdamW(
        model.parameters(),
        lr=1e-3,
        betas=(0.9, 0.98),
        weight_decay=0.5,
    )
    num_train_batches = 10
    num_eval_batches = 8
    num_total_updates = 7500
    warmup_steps = 50
    scheduler = torch.optim.lr_scheduler.LambdaLR(
        optimizer, lr_lambda=lambda s: min(s / warmup_steps, 1)
    )

    final_info, train_log_info, val_log_info = [], [], []
    step_val_acc_99 = num_total_updates
    invariance_scores = []
    max_invariance_score = 0
    invariance_point = num_total_updates
    for ep in range(num_total_updates // num_train_batches):
        train_metrics = train(
            model,
            train_loader,
            optimizer,
            scheduler,
            device,
            num_train_batches,
        )
        val_metrics = evaluate(
            model,
            val_loader,
            device,
            num_eval_batches,
        )
        train_metrics["step"] = (ep + 1) * num_train_batches
        val_metrics["step"] = (ep + 1) * num_train_batches

        if (ep + 1) * num_train_batches % 100 == 0:
            inv_score = invariance_score(model, dataset_obj, device)
            invariance_scores.append({"step": (ep + 1) * num_train_batches, "score": inv_score})
            if inv_score > max_invariance_score:
                max_invariance_score = inv_score
            if invariance_point == num_total_updates and inv_score > 0.9 * max_invariance_score:
                invariance_point = (ep + 1) * num_train_batches

        if step_val_acc_99 == num_total_updates and val_metrics["val_accuracy"] > 0.99:
            step_val_acc_99 = val_metrics["step"]
        train_log_info.append(train_metrics)
        val_log_info.append(val_metrics)

    final_info = {
        "final_train_loss": train_metrics["train_loss"],
        "final_val_loss": val_metrics["val_loss"],
        "final_train_acc": train_metrics["train_accuracy"],
        "final_val_acc": val_metrics["val_accuracy"],
        "step_val_acc_99": step_val_acc_99,
        "invariance_point": invariance_point,
    }
    print(final_info)
    with open(
        os.path.join(out_dir, f"final_info_{dataset}_{seed_offset}.json"), "w"
    ) as f:
        json.dump(final_info, f)
    
    # Save invariance scores
    with open(
        os.path.join(out_dir, f"invariance_scores_{dataset}_{seed_offset}.json"), "w"
    ) as f:
        json.dump(invariance_scores, f)
    
    return final_info, train_log_info, val_log_info, invariance_scores
=======
def visualize_attention(model, dataset, device, step, out_dir):
    model.eval()
    attention_patterns = []
    
    for _ in range(5):  # Get 5 random examples
        x, y, _ = dataset.fetch_val_example()
        x = torch.tensor(x).unsqueeze(0).to(device)
        
        with torch.no_grad():
            output = model(x)
            # Assuming the last layer's attention is what we want to visualize
            attention = model.model[0].self_attn.attention_weights.squeeze(0).cpu().numpy()
        
        attention_patterns.append(attention)
    
    # Plot attention patterns
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 5, figsize=(20, 4))
    for i, attn in enumerate(attention_patterns):
        sns.heatmap(attn, ax=axes[i], cmap='viridis')
        axes[i].set_title(f'Example {i+1}')
    
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig(os.path.join(out_dir, f'attention_pattern_step_{step}.png'))
    plt.close()

def run(out_dir, dataset, seed_offset):
    os.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)
    device = torch.device("cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")
    torch.manual_seed(1337 + seed_offset)
    train_loader, val_loader, n_vocab, n_output = get_data(
        operation=dataset,
        prime=97,
        training_fraction=0.5,
        batch_size=512,
    )

    # Create the dataset object
    dataset_obj = operation_mod_p_data(dataset, 97, 0.5)

    model = Transformer(
        num_layers=2,
        dim_model=128,
        num_heads=4,
        vocab_size=n_vocab,
        output_size=n_output,
        seq_len=5,
    ).to(device)

    optimizer = torch.optim.AdamW(
        model.parameters(),
        lr=1e-3,
        betas=(0.9, 0.98),
        weight_decay=0.5,
    )
    num_train_batches = 10
    num_eval_batches = 8
    num_total_updates = 7500
    warmup_steps = 50
    scheduler = torch.optim.lr_scheduler.LambdaLR(
        optimizer, lr_lambda=lambda s: min(s / warmup_steps, 1)
    )

    final_info, train_log_info, val_log_info = [], [], []
    step_val_acc_99 = num_total_updates
    invariance_scores = []
    max_invariance_score = 0
    invariance_point = num_total_updates
    grokking_point = num_total_updates
    
    # Visualize attention at the start (pre-grokking)
    visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device, 0, out_dir)
    
    for ep in range(num_total_updates // num_train_batches):
        train_metrics = train(
            model,
            train_loader,
            optimizer,
            scheduler,
            device,
            num_train_batches,
        )
        val_metrics = evaluate(
            model,
            val_loader,
            device,
            num_eval_batches,
        )
        train_metrics["step"] = (ep + 1) * num_train_batches
        val_metrics["step"] = (ep + 1) * num_train_batches

        if (ep + 1) * num_train_batches % 100 == 0:
            inv_score = invariance_score(model, dataset_obj, device)
            invariance_scores.append({"step": (ep + 1) * num_train_batches, "score": inv_score})
            if inv_score > max_invariance_score:
                max_invariance_score = inv_score
            if invariance_point == num_total_updates and inv_score > 0.9 * max_invariance_score:
                invariance_point = (ep + 1) * num_train_batches
                # Visualize attention at invariance point
                visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device, invariance_point, out_dir)

        if step_val_acc_99 == num_total_updates and val_metrics["val_accuracy"] > 0.99:
            step_val_acc_99 = val_metrics["step"]
            grokking_point = step_val_acc_99
            # Visualize attention at grokking point
            visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device, grokking_point, out_dir)
        
        train_log_info.append(train_metrics)
        val_log_info.append(val_metrics)

    # Visualize attention at the end (post-grokking)
    visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device, num_total_updates, out_dir)

    final_info = {
        "final_train_loss": train_metrics["train_loss"],
        "final_val_loss": val_metrics["val_loss"],
        "final_train_acc": train_metrics["train_accuracy"],
        "final_val_acc": val_metrics["val_accuracy"],
        "step_val_acc_99": step_val_acc_99,
        "invariance_point": invariance_point,
        "grokking_point": grokking_point,
    }
    print(final_info)
    with open(
        os.path.join(out_dir, f"final_info_{dataset}_{seed_offset}.json"), "w"
    ) as f:
        json.dump(final_info, f)

    with open(
        os.path.join(out_dir, f"invariance_scores_{dataset}_{seed_offset}.json"), "w"
    ) as f:
        json.dump(invariance_scores, f)
    
    return final_info, train_log_info, val_log_info, invariance_scores
```

Now, we need to modify the `Transformer` class to store attention weights:

```python
<<<<<<< SEARCH
class DecoderBlock(torch.nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, dim_model: int, n_heads: int):
        super().__init__()

        self.self_attn = nn.MultiheadAttention(dim_model, n_heads)
        self.self_attn_norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim_model)
        self.ffn = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(dim_model, dim_model * 4),
            nn.GELU(),
            nn.Linear(dim_model * 4, dim_model),
        )
        self.ffn_norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim_model)

    def forward(self, x: Tensor):
        attn_mask = torch.full(
            (len(x), len(x)), -float("Inf"), device=x.device, dtype=x.dtype
        )
        attn_mask = torch.triu(attn_mask, diagonal=1)

        a1, _ = self.self_attn(x, x, x, attn_mask=attn_mask)
        a1 = self.self_attn_norm(x + a1)
        a2 = self.ffn(a1)
        a2 = self.ffn_norm(a1 + a2)

        return a2
=======
class DecoderBlock(torch.nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, dim_model: int, n_heads: int):
        super().__init__()

        self.self_attn = nn.MultiheadAttention(dim_model, n_heads)
        self.self_attn_norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim_model)
        self.ffn = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(dim_model, dim_model * 4),
            nn.GELU(),
            nn.Linear(dim_model * 4, dim_model),
        )
        self.ffn_norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim_model)
        self.attention_weights = None

    def forward(self, x: Tensor):
        attn_mask = torch.full(
            (len(x), len(x)), -float("Inf"), device=x.device, dtype=x.dtype
        )
        attn_mask = torch.triu(attn_mask, diagonal=1)

        a1, attention_weights = self.self_attn(x, x, x, attn_mask=attn_mask)
        self.attention_weights = attention_weights
        a1 = self.self_attn_norm(x + a1)
        a2 = self.ffn(a1)
        a2 = self.ffn_norm(a1 + a2)

        return a2
```

I understand the error. The issue is that we're trying to access the            
attention_weights attribute of the MultiheadAttention module, which doesn't     
exist by default. We need to modify our DecoderBlock class to store the         
attention weights and then access them in the visualize_attention function.     
Let's make the necessary changes:

 class DecoderBlock(torch.nn.Module):                                           
     def __init__(self, dim_model: int, n_heads: int):                          
         super().__init__()                                                     
                                                                                
         self.self_attn = nn.MultiheadAttention(dim_model, n_heads)             
         self.self_attn_norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim_model)                          
         self.ffn = nn.Sequential(                                              
             nn.Linear(dim_model, dim_model * 4),                               
             nn.GELU(),                                                         
             nn.Linear(dim_model * 4, dim_model),                               
         )                                                                      
         self.ffn_norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim_model)                                
         self.attention_weights = None                                          
                                                                                
     def forward(self, x: Tensor):                                              
         attn_mask = torch.full(                                                
             (len(x), len(x)), -float("Inf"), device=x.device, dtype=x.dtype    
         )                                                                      
         attn_mask = torch.triu(attn_mask, diagonal=1)                          
                                                                                
         a1, attention_weights = self.self_attn(x, x, x, attn_mask=attn_mask)   
         self.attention_weights = attention_weights                             
         a1 = self.self_attn_norm(x + a1)                                       
         a2 = self.ffn(a1)                                                      
         a2 = self.ffn_norm(a1 + a2)                                            
                                                                                
         return a2

 class DecoderBlock(torch.nn.Module):                                           
     def __init__(self, dim_model: int, n_heads: int):                          
         super().__init__()                                                     
                                                                                
         self.self_attn = nn.MultiheadAttention(dim_model, n_heads)

             nn.Linear(dim_model, dim_model * 4),                               
             nn.GELU(),                                                         
             nn.Linear(dim_model * 4, dim_model),                               
         )                                                                      
         self.ffn_norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim_model)                                
         self.attention_weights = None                                          
                                                                                
     def forward(self, x: Tensor):                                              
         attn_mask = torch.full(                                                
             (len(x), len(x)), -float("Inf"), device=x.device, dtype=x.dtype    
         )                                                                      
         attn_mask = torch.triu(attn_mask, diagonal=1)

I understand the error. The issue is that we're trying to access the `attention_weights` attribute of the `MultiheadAttention` module, which doesn't exist by default. We need to modify our `DecoderBlock` class to store the attention weights and then access them in the `visualize_attention` function. Let's make the necessary changes:

experiment.py
```python
<<<<<<< SEARCH
class DecoderBlock(torch.nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, dim_model: int, n_heads: int):
        super().__init__()

        self.self_attn = nn.MultiheadAttention(dim_model, n_heads)
        self.self_attn_norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim_model)
        self.ffn = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(dim_model, dim_model * 4),
            nn.GELU(),
            nn.Linear(dim_model * 4, dim_model),
        )
        self.ffn_norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim_model)
        self.attention_weights = None

    def forward(self, x: Tensor):
        attn_mask = torch.full(
            (len(x), len(x)), -float("Inf"), device=x.device, dtype=x.dtype
        )
        attn_mask = torch.triu(attn_mask, diagonal=1)

        a1, attention_weights = self.self_attn(x, x, x, attn_mask=attn_mask)
        self.attention_weights = attention_weights
        a1 = self.self_attn_norm(x + a1)
        a2 = self.ffn(a1)
        a2 = self.ffn_norm(a1 + a2)

        return a2
=======
class DecoderBlock(torch.nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, dim_model: int, n_heads: int):
        super().__init__()

        self.self_attn = nn.MultiheadAttention(dim_model, n_heads)
        self.self_attn_norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim_model)
        self.ffn = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(dim_model, dim_model * 4),
            nn.GELU(),
            nn.Linear(dim_model * 4, dim_model),
        )
        self.ffn_norm = nn.LayerNorm(dim_model)
        self.attention_weights = None

    def forward(self, x: Tensor):
        attn_mask = torch.full(
            (len(x), len(x)), -float("Inf"), device=x.device, dtype=x.dtype
        )
        attn_mask = torch.triu(attn_mask, diagonal=1)

        a1, attention_weights = self.self_attn(x, x, x, attn_mask=attn_mask, need_weights=True)
        self.attention_weights = attention_weights
        a1 = self.self_attn_norm(x + a1)
        a2 = self.ffn(a1)
        a2 = self.ffn_norm(a1 + a2)

        return a2
```

```python
def visualize_attention(model, dataset, device, step, out_dir):
    model.eval()
    attention_patterns = []
    
    for _ in range(5):  # Get 5 random examples
        x, y, _ = dataset.fetch_val_example()
        x = torch.tensor(x).unsqueeze(0).to(device)
        
        with torch.no_grad():
            output = model(x)
            # Assuming the last layer's attention is what we want to visualize
            attention = model.model[0].self_attn.attention_weights.squeeze(0).cpu().numpy()
        
        attention_patterns.append(attention)
    
    # Plot attention patterns
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 5, figsize=(20, 4))
    for i, attn in enumerate(attention_patterns):
        sns.heatmap(attn, ax=axes[i], cmap='viridis')
        axes[i].set_title(f'Example {i+1}')
    
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig(os.path.join(out_dir, f'attention_pattern_step_{step}.png'))
    plt.close()

def visualize_attention(model, dataset, device, step, out_dir):
    model.eval()
    attention_patterns = []
    
    for _ in range(5):  # Get 5 random examples
        x, y, _ = dataset.fetch_val_example()
        x = torch.tensor(x).unsqueeze(0).to(device)
        
        with torch.no_grad():
            output = model(x)
            # Assuming the last layer's attention is what we want to visualize
            attention = model.model[0].attention_weights.squeeze(0).cpu().numpy()
        
        attention_patterns.append(attention)
    
    # Plot attention patterns
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 5, figsize=(20, 4))
    for i, attn in enumerate(attention_patterns):
        sns.heatmap(attn, ax=axes[i], cmap='viridis')
        axes[i].set_title(f'Example {i+1}')
    
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig(os.path.join(out_dir, f'attention_pattern_step_{step}.png'))
    plt.close()
```

These changes should resolve the AttributeError and allow the attention visualization to work correctly. The main modifications are:

1. In the `DecoderBlock` class, we added `need_weights=True` to the `self_attn` call to ensure that attention weights are returned.
2. In the `visualize_attention` function, we changed how we access the attention weights to match our `DecoderBlock` implementation.

These modifications should allow the experiment to run without the AttributeError.

 Observations:                                                                  
 1. Invariance Point: For all datasets, the invariance point is consistently    
 reached very early in training (step 100). This suggests that the model quickl 
 learns the task-specific invariances, regardless of the operation type.        
                                                                                
 2. Grokking Point:                                                             
    - x_div_y: Reaches 99% validation accuracy at step 4276 (mean), showing a   
 significant delay between invariance learning and generalization.              
    - x_minus_y: Similar to x_div_y, with 99% validation accuracy at step 4533  
 (mean).                                                                        
    - x_plus_y: Fastest to reach high validation accuracy, with 99% at step 248 
 (mean).                                                                        
    - permutation: Fails to reach high validation accuracy within the 7500 step 
 indicating difficulty in generalizing for this task.                           
                                                                                
 3. Final Performance:                                                          
    - x_div_y, x_minus_y, and x_plus_y all achieve perfect or near-perfect      
 validation accuracy by the end of training.                                    
    - The permutation task shows poor generalization, with final validation     
 accuracy at only 11.98%.                                                       
                                                                                
 4. Training Dynamics:                                                          
    - The arithmetic operations (division, subtraction, addition) show similar  
 patterns of rapid invariance learning followed by a period of low validation   
 accuracy, then sudden improvement (grokking).                                  
    - The permutation task exhibits a different pattern, with rapid invariance  
 learning but failure to achieve high validation accuracy.                      
                                                                                
 5. Correlation between Invariance Score and Validation Accuracy:               
    - For the arithmetic operations, there is a significant time gap between    
 achieving high invariance scores and high validation accuracy.                 
    - This suggests that while invariance learning is necessary for             
 generalization, it is not sufficient on its own to guarantee high performance  
 the validation set.                                                            
                                                                                
 Next steps:                                                                    
 1. Analyze the attention patterns at key points in training (pre-grokking,     
 invariance point, grokking point, post-grokking) to understand how learned     
 invariances manifest in the model's attention mechanisms.                      
 2. Investigate why the permutation task is particularly challenging for the    
 model to generalize, despite learning invariances quickly.                     
 3. Experiment with different model architectures or training regimes to see if 
 we can reduce the gap between invariance learning and generalization.

 =======                                                                        
 def visualize_attention(model, dataset, device, step, out_dir):                
     model.eval()                                                               
     attention_patterns = []                                                    
     inputs = []                                                                
     outputs = []                                                               
                                                                                
     for _ in range(5):  # Get 5 random examples                                
         x, y, equation = dataset.fetch_val_example()                           
         x = torch.tensor(x).unsqueeze(0).to(device)                            
                                                                                
         with torch.no_grad():                                                  
             output = model(x)                                                  
             # Get attention weights from all layers                            
             attention = [layer.attention_weights.squeeze(0).cpu().numpy() for  
 layer in model.model if hasattr(layer, 'attention_weights')]                   
                                                                                
         attention_patterns.append(attention)                                   
         inputs.append(equation[:-1])  # Input equation without the result      
         outputs.append(dataset.idx2vocab[y + 2])  # Convert output index to

 1. We can now analyze the visualizations of the metrics over time to gain deep 
 insights into the learning process for each dataset.                           
                                                                                
 Observations:                                                                  
 1. Invariance Point: For all datasets, the invariance point is consistently    
 reached very early in training (step 100). This suggests that the model quickl 
 learns the task-specific invariances, regardless of the operation type.        
                                                                                
 2. Grokking Point:                                                             
    - x_div_y: Reaches 99% validation accuracy at step 4276 (mean), showing a   
 significant delay between invariance learning and generalization.              
    - x_minus_y: Similar to x_div_y, with 99% validation accuracy at step 4533  
 (mean).                                                                        
    - x_plus_y: Fastest to reach high validation accuracy, with 99% at step 248 
 (mean).                                                                        
    - permutation: Fails to reach high validation accuracy within the 7500 step 
 indicating difficulty in generalizing for this task.

    - x_div_y, x_minus_y, and x_plus_y all achieve perfect or near-perfect      
 validation accuracy by the end of training.                                    
    - The permutation task shows poor generalization, with final validation     
 accuracy at only 11.98%.                                                       
                                                                                
 4. Training Dynamics:                                                          
    - The arithmetic operations (division, subtraction, addition) show similar  
 patterns of rapid invariance learning followed by a period of low validation   
 accuracy, then sudden improvement (grokking).                                  
    - The permutation task exhibits a different pattern, with rapid invariance  
 learning but failure to achieve high validation accuracy.                      
                                                                                
 5. Correlation between Invariance Score and Validation Accuracy:               
    - For the arithmetic operations, there is a significant time gap between    
 achieving high invariance scores and high validation accuracy.                 
    - This suggests that while invariance learning is necessary for             
 generalization, it is not sufficient on its own to guarantee high performance  
 the validation set.                                                            
                                                                                
 Next steps:                                                                    
 1. Analyze the attention patterns at key points in training (pre-grokking,     
 invariance point, grokking point, post-grokking) to understand how learned     
 invariances manifest in the model's attention mechanisms.                      
 2. Investigate why the permutation task is particularly challenging for the    
 model to generalize, despite learning invariances quickly.                     
 3. Experiment with different model architectures or training regimes to see if 
 we can reduce the gap between invariance learning and generalization.

 def visualize_attention(model, dataset, device, step, out_dir):                
     model.eval()                                                               
     attention_patterns = []                                                    
                                                                                
     for _ in range(5):  # Get 5 random examples                                
         x, y, _ = dataset.fetch_val_example()                                  
         x = torch.tensor(x).unsqueeze(0).to(device)                            
                                                                                
         with torch.no_grad():                                                  
             output = model(x)                                                  
             # Assuming the last layer's attention is what we want to visualize 
             attention =                                                        
 model.model[0].attention_weights.squeeze(0).cpu().numpy()                      
                                                                                
         attention_patterns.append(attention)                                   
                                                                                
     # Plot attention patterns                                                  
     fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 5, figsize=(20, 4))                            
     for i, attn in enumerate(attention_patterns):                              
         sns.heatmap(attn, ax=axes[i], cmap='viridis')                          
         axes[i].set_title(f'Example {i+1}')                                    
                                                                                
     plt.tight_layout()                                                         
     plt.savefig(os.path.join(out_dir, f'attention_pattern_step_{step}.png'))   
     plt.close()                                                                
 =======                                                                        
 def visualize_attention(model, dataset, device, step, out_dir):                
     model.eval()                                                               
     attention_patterns = []                                                    
     inputs = []                                                                
     outputs = []                                                               
                                                                                
     for _ in range(5):  # Get 5 random examples                                
         x, y, equation = dataset.fetch_val_example()                           
         x = torch.tensor(x).unsqueeze(0).to(device)                            
                                                                                
         with torch.no_grad():                                                  
             output = model(x)                                                  
             # Get attention weights from all layers                            
             attention = [layer.attention_weights.squeeze(0).cpu().numpy() for  
 layer in model.model if hasattr(layer, 'attention_weights')]                   
                                                                                
         attention_patterns.append(attention)                                   
         inputs.append(equation[:-1])  # Input equation without the result      
         outputs.append(dataset.idx2vocab[y + 2])  # Convert output index to    
 vocabulary                                                                     
                                                                                
     # Plot attention patterns                                                  
     fig, axes = plt.subplots(len(attention_patterns), len(attention[0]),       
 figsize=(20, 4*len(attention_patterns)))                                       
     for i, (attn, inp, out) in enumerate(zip(attention_patterns, inputs,       
 outputs)):                                                                     
         for j,layer_attn in enumerate(attn):                                   
             sns.heatmap(layer_attn, ax=axes[i,j] if len(attention_patterns) >  
 else axes[j], cmap='viridis')                                                  
             axes[i,j].set_title(f'Layer {j+1}, Input: {inp}, Output: {out}' if 
 len(attention_patterns) > 1 else f'Example {i+1}, Layer {j+1}\nInput: {inp},   
 Output: {out}')                                                                
                                                                                
     plt.tight_layout()                                                         
     plt.savefig(os.path.join(out_dir, f'attention_pattern_step_{step}.png'))   
     plt.close()                                                                
                                                                                
 def analyze_permutation_task(model, dataset, device, out_dir):                 
     model.eval()                                                               
     correct = 0                                                                
     total = 0                                                                  
     attention_patterns = []                                                    
                                                                                
     for _ in range(100):  # Analyze 100 examples                               
         x, y, equation = dataset.fetch_val_example()                           
         x = torch.tensor(x).unsqueeze(0).to(device)                            
         y = torch.tensor(y).to(device)                                         
                                                                                
         with torch.no_grad():                                                  
             output = model(x)                                                  
             pred = torch.argmax(output[-1, :, :], dim=1)                       
             correct += (pred == y).sum().item()                                
             total += 1                                                         
                                                                                
             # Get attention weights from all layers                            
             attention = [layer.attention_weights.squeeze(0).cpu().numpy() for  
 layer in model.model if hasattr(layer, 'attention_weights')]                   
             attention_patterns.append((attention, equation, dataset.idx2vocab[ 
 + 2], dataset.idx2vocab[pred.item() + 2]))                                     
                                                                                
     accuracy = correct / total                                                 
                                                                                
     # Analyze attention patterns                                               
     avg_attention = [np.mean([pattern[0][i] for pattern in attention_patterns] 
 axis=0) for i in range(len(attention_patterns[0][0]))]                         
                                                                                
     # Plot average attention patterns                                          
     fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, len(avg_attention), figsize=(20, 4))           
     for i, attn in enumerate(avg_attention):                                   
         sns.heatmap(attn, ax=axes[i], cmap='viridis')                          
         axes[i].set_title(f'Layer {i+1} Average Attention')                    
                                                                                
     plt.tight_layout()                                                         
     plt.savefig(os.path.join(out_dir, 'permutation_avg_attention.png'))        
     plt.close()                                                                
                                                                                
     # Analyze correct vs incorrect predictions                                 
     correct_patterns = [pattern for pattern in attention_patterns if pattern[2 
 == pattern[3]]                                                                 
     incorrect_patterns = [pattern for pattern in attention_patterns if         
 pattern[2] != pattern[3]]                                                      
                                                                                
     with open(os.path.join(out_dir, 'permutation_analysis.txt'), 'w') as f:    
         f.write(f"Permutation Task Analysis\n")                                
         f.write(f"Accuracy: {accuracy:.2f}\n\n")                               
         f.write(f"Number of correct predictions: {len(correct_patterns)}\n")   
         f.write(f"Number of incorrect predictions:                             
 {len(incorrect_patterns)}\n\n")                                                
         f.write("Sample of correct predictions:\n")                            
         for pattern in correct_patterns[:5]:                                   
             f.write(f"Input: {pattern[1]}, True Output: {pattern[2]}, Predicte 
 Output: {pattern[3]}\n")                                                       
         f.write("\nSample of incorrect predictions:\n")                        
         for pattern in incorrect_patterns[:5]:                                 
             f.write(f"Input: {pattern[1]}, True Output: {pattern[2]}, Predicte 
 Output: {pattern[3]}\n")                                                       
                                                                                
     return accuracy, avg_attention

 def run(out_dir, dataset, seed_offset):                                        
     os.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)                                        
     device = torch.device("cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")      
     torch.manual_seed(1337 + seed_offset)                                      
     train_loader, val_loader, n_vocab, n_output = get_data(                    
         operation=dataset,                                                     
         prime=97,                                                              
         training_fraction=0.5,                                                 
         batch_size=512,                                                        
     )                                                                          
                                                                                
     # Create the dataset object                                                
     dataset_obj = operation_mod_p_data(dataset, 97, 0.5)                       
                                                                                
     model = Transformer(                                                       
         num_layers=2,                                                          
         dim_model=128,                                                         
         num_heads=4,                                                           
         vocab_size=n_vocab,                                                    
         output_size=n_output,                                                  
         seq_len=5,                                                             
     ).to(device)                                                               
                                                                                
     optimizer = torch.optim.AdamW(                                             
         model.parameters(),                                                    
         lr=1e-3,                                                               
         betas=(0.9, 0.98),                                                     
         weight_decay=0.5,                                                      
     )                                                                          
     num_train_batches = 10                                                     
     num_eval_batches = 8                                                       
     num_total_updates = 7500                                                   
     warmup_steps = 50                                                          
     scheduler = torch.optim.lr_scheduler.LambdaLR(                             
         optimizer, lr_lambda=lambda s: min(s / warmup_steps, 1)                
     )                                                                          
                                                                                
     final_info, train_log_info, val_log_info = [], [], []                      
     step_val_acc_99 = num_total_updates                                        
     invariance_scores = []                                                     
     max_invariance_score = 0                                                   
     invariance_point = num_total_updates                                       
     grokking_point = num_total_updates                                         
                                                                                
     # Visualize attention at the start (pre-grokking)                          
     visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device, 0, out_dir)                
                                                                                
     for ep in range(num_total_updates // num_train_batches):                   
         train_metrics = train(                                                 
             model,                                                             
             train_loader,                                                      
             optimizer,                                                         
             scheduler,                                                         
             device,                                                            
             num_train_batches,                                                 
         )                                                                      
         val_metrics = evaluate(                                                
             model,                                                             
             val_loader,                                                        
             device,                                                            
             num_eval_batches,                                                  
         )                                                                      
         train_metrics["step"] = (ep + 1) * num_train_batches                   
         val_metrics["step"] = (ep + 1) * num_train_batches                     
                                                                                
         if (ep + 1) * num_train_batches % 100 == 0:                            
             inv_score = invariance_score(model, dataset_obj, device)           
             invariance_scores.append({"step": (ep + 1) * num_train_batches,    
 "score": inv_score})                                                           
             if inv_score > max_invariance_score:                               
                 max_invariance_score = inv_score                               
             if invariance_point == num_total_updates and inv_score > 0.9 *     
 max_invariance_score:                                                          
                 invariance_point = (ep + 1) * num_train_batches                
                 # Visualize attention at invariance point                      
                 visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device,                
 invariance_point, out_dir)                                                     
                                                                                
         if step_val_acc_99 == num_total_updates and val_metrics["val_accuracy" 
 > 0.99:                                                                        
             step_val_acc_99 = val_metrics["step"]                              
             grokking_point = step_val_acc_99                                   
             # Visualize attention at grokking point                            
             visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device, grokking_point,    
 out_dir)                                                                       
                                                                                
         train_log_info.append(train_metrics)                                   
         val_log_info.append(val_metrics)                                       
                                                                                
     # Visualize attention at the end (post-grokking)                           
     visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device, num_total_updates, out_dir 
                                                                                
     final_info = {                                                             
         "final_train_loss": train_metrics["train_loss"],                       
         "final_val_loss": val_metrics["val_loss"],                             
         "final_train_acc": train_metrics["train_accuracy"],                    
         "final_val_acc": val_metrics["val_accuracy"],                          
         "step_val_acc_99": step_val_acc_99,                                    
         "invariance_point": invariance_point,                                  
         "grokking_point": grokking_point,                                      
     }                                                                          
     print(final_info)                                                          
     with open(                                                                 
         os.path.join(out_dir, f"final_info_{dataset}_{seed_offset}.json"), "w" 
     ) as f:                                                                    
         json.dump(final_info, f)                                               
                                                                                
     # Save invariance scores                                                   
     with open(                                                                 
         os.path.join(out_dir,                                                  
 f"invariance_scores_{dataset}_{seed_offset}.json"), "w"                        
     ) as f:                                                                    
         json.dump(invariance_scores, f)                                        
                                                                                
     return final_info, train_log_info, val_log_info, invariance_scores         
 =======                                                                        
 def run(out_dir, dataset, seed_offset):                                        
     os.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)                                        
     device = torch.device("cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")      
     torch.manual_seed(1337 + seed_offset)                                      
     train_loader, val_loader, n_vocab, n_output = get_data(                    
         operation=dataset,                                                     
         prime=97,                                                              
         training_fraction=0.5,                                                 
         batch_size=512,                                                        
     )                                                                          
                                                                                
     # Create the dataset object                                                
     dataset_obj = operation_mod_p_data(dataset, 97, 0.5)                       
                                                                                
     model = Transformer(                                                       
         num_layers=2,                                                          
         dim_model=128,                                                         
         num_heads=4,                                                           
         vocab_size=n_vocab,                                                    
         output_size=n_output,                                                  
         seq_len=5,                                                             
     ).to(device)                                                               
                                                                                
     optimizer = torch.optim.AdamW(                                             
         model.parameters(),                                                    
         lr=1e-3,                                                               
         betas=(0.9, 0.98),                                                     
         weight_decay=0.5,                                                      
     )                                                                          
     num_train_batches = 10                                                     
     num_eval_batches = 8                                                       
     num_total_updates = 7500                                                   
     warmup_steps = 50                                                          
     scheduler = torch.optim.lr_scheduler.LambdaLR(                             
         optimizer, lr_lambda=lambda s: min(s / warmup_steps, 1)                
     )                                                                          
                                                                                
     final_info, train_log_info, val_log_info = [], [], []                      
     step_val_acc_99 = num_total_updates                                        
     invariance_scores = []                                                     
     max_invariance_score = 0                                                   
     invariance_point = num_total_updates                                       
     grokking_point = num_total_updates                                         
                                                                                
     # Visualize attention at the start (pre-grokking)                          
     visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device, 0, out_dir)                
                                                                                
     for ep in range(num_total_updates // num_train_batches):                   
         train_metrics = train(                                                 
             model,                                                             
             train_loader,                                                      
             optimizer,                                                         
             scheduler,                                                         
             device,                                                            
             num_train_batches,                                                 
         )                                                                      
         val_metrics = evaluate(                                                
             model,                                                             
             val_loader,                                                        
             device,                                                            
             num_eval_batches,                                                  
         )                                                                      
         train_metrics["step"] = (ep + 1) * num_train_batches                   
         val_metrics["step"] = (ep + 1) * num_train_batches                     
                                                                                
         if (ep + 1) * num_train_batches % 100 == 0:                            
             inv_score = invariance_score(model, dataset_obj, device)           
             invariance_scores.append({"step": (ep + 1) * num_train_batches,    
 "score": inv_score})                                                           
             if inv_score > max_invariance_score:                               
                 max_invariance_score = inv_score                               
             if invariance_point == num_total_updates and inv_score > 0.9 *     
 max_invariance_score:                                                          
                 invariance_point = (ep + 1) * num_train_batches                
                 # Visualize attention at invariance point                      
                 visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device,                
 invariance_point, out_dir)                                                     
                                                                                
         if step_val_acc_99 == num_total_updates and val_metrics["val_accuracy" 
 > 0.99:                                                                        
             step_val_acc_99 = val_metrics["step"]                              
             grokking_point = step_val_acc_99                                   
             # Visualize attention at grokking point                            
             visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device, grokking_point,    
 out_dir)                                                                       
                                                                                
         train_log_info.append(train_metrics)                                   
         val_log_info.append(val_metrics)                                       
                                                                                
     # Visualize attention at the end (post-grokking)                           
     visualize_attention(model, dataset_obj, device, num_total_updates, out_dir 
                                                                                
     final_info = {                                                             
         "final_train_loss": train_metrics["train_loss"],                       
         "final_val_loss": val_metrics["val_loss"],                             
         "final_train_acc": train_metrics["train_accuracy"],                    
         "final_val_acc": val_metrics["val_accuracy"],                          
         "step_val_acc_99": step_val_acc_99,                                    
         "invariance_point": invariance_point,                                  
         "grokking_point": grokking_point,                                      
     }                                                                          
                                                                                
     # Perform detailed analysis for permutation task                           
     if dataset == "permutation":                                               
         perm_accuracy, avg_attention = analyze_permutation_task(model,         
 dataset_obj, device, out_dir)                                                  
         final_info["permutation_analysis"] = {                                 
             "accuracy": perm_accuracy,                                         
             "avg_attention": [attn.tolist() for attn in avg_attention]         
         }                                                                      
                                                                                
     print(final_info)                                                          
     with open(                                                                 
         os.path.join(out_dir, f"final_info_{dataset}_{seed_offset}.json"), "w" 
     ) as f:                                                                    
         json.dump(final_info, f)                                               
                                                                                
     # Save invariance scores                                                   
     with open(                                                                 
         os.path.join(out_dir,                                                  
 f"invariance_scores_{dataset}_{seed_offset}.json"), "w"                        
     ) as f:                                                                    
         json.dump(invariance_scores, f)                                        
                                                                                
     return final_info, train_log_info, val_log_info, invariance_scores

```python
1. Invariance Point: For all datasets, the invariance point is consistently reached very early in training (step 100). This suggests that the model quickly learns the task-specific invariances, regardless of the operation type.

   - The permutation task shows poor generalization, with final validation accuracy at only 11.98%.

4. Training Dynamics:
   - The arithmetic operations (division, subtraction, addition) show similar patterns of rapid invariance learning followed by a period of low validation accuracy, then sudden improvement (grokking).
   - The permutation task exhibits a different pattern, with rapid invariance learning but failure to achieve high validation accuracy.

5. Correlation between Invariance Score and Validation Accuracy:
   - For the arithmetic operations, there is a significant time gap between achieving high invariance scores and high validation accuracy.
   - This suggests that while invariance learning is necessary for generalization, it is not sufficient on its own to guarantee high performance on the validation set.
```
